# Proposed OEIS comments: A207113 to A208074

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A207113

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207113>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [6365](../papers/06001-06500/06365-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) +5*a(n-2) -4*a(n-3) -23*a(n-4) +4*a(n-5) +40*a(n-6) +19*a(n-7) -38*a(n-8) -28*a(n-9) -a(n-11) +13*a(n-12) +38*a(n-13) +15*a(n-14) -31*a(n-15) -24*a(n-16) -2*a(n-17) +9*a(n-18) +6*a(n-19) +6*a(n-20) -2*a(n-21) -3*a(n-22) -a(n-23) +a(n-24)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 24. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207142

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207142>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10222](../papers/10001-10500/10222-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 2*a(n-2) - 2*a(n-3) for n>8.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 19 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207165

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207165>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3378](../papers/03001-03500/03378-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*n^3 + 9*n - 9.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 19 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 91 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 21 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A207449

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207449>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3371](../papers/03001-03500/03371-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 10*n^3 + 10*n^2 - 10*n.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 22 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 111 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207596

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207596>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3342](../papers/03001-03500/03342-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 30*n^3 + 15*n^2 - 45*n + 15.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 25 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 241 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---
